(4.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega_{\varepsilon}(x,t)(\Phi\circ X_{t}^{-1})(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\omega_{\varepsilon}^{0}(x)\Phi(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Also, it clearly holds that

(4.9)

\Phi\circ X_{t}^{-1}=\mathds{1}_{X_{t}(A(a,b,l_{1},l_{2}))}\,,

and that

(4.10)

X_{t}(A(a,b,l_{1},l_{2}))=\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}X_{t}\left(\bigl\{\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s)\,|\,s\in[a,b]\bigr\}\right)=\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{X_{t}(\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s))\,|\,s\in[a,b]\bigr\}\,.

Now, using that the curves
\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t)
are transported by the flow by definition, and using the reparametrization map
\sigma
, defined in (
2.12
) and (
2.13
), we can further rewrite this set as

(4.11)

\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{X_{t}(\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s))\,|\,s\in[a,b]\bigr\}=\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(s),t)\,|\,s\in[a,b]\bigr\}\,,

where the inverse of
\sigma_{t}
is taken with respect to the variable
s
, and
\sigma_{t}
is invertible by the assumption that the curves are graphs over
\Gamma
, as explained in (
2.12
)–(
2.13
) above. The function
\sigma_{t}^{-1}
is monotone (otherwise it cannot be invertible) and hence maps
[a,b]
to
[\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)]
bijectively. Thus, we have that

(4.12)

\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(s),t)\,|\,s\in[a,b]\bigr\}=\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t)\,|\,\tilde{s}\in[\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)]\bigr\}\,.

In sum, we have obtained that

(4.13)

\int_{\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t)\,|\,s\in[\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)]\bigr\}}\omega_{\varepsilon}(x,t)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\bigcup_{l\in[l_{1},l_{2}]}\bigl\{\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s)\,|\,s\in[a,b]\bigr\}}\omega_{\varepsilon}^{0}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Rewriting this equation equation in terms of the densities
\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}
and using the definitions (
2.16
) and (
2.17
), this equation reads as

(4.14)

\int_{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}^{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)}\int_{l_{1}}^{l_{2}}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t){\,\mathrm{d}}s{\,\mathrm{d}}l=\int_{a}^{b}\int_{l_{1}}^{l_{2}}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s){\,\mathrm{d}}s{\,\mathrm{d}}l\,.

We can now let
l_{1}-l_{2}\rightarrow 0
and see from the continuity of the solution that

(4.15)

\int_{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}^{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t){\,\mathrm{d}}s=\int_{a}^{b}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s){\,\mathrm{d}}s\,,

where
l
denotes the common limit of
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
. In other words, the integral on the left hand side is a conserved quantity for every
l\in[-1,1]
and for all
a,b\in\mathbb{T}
. Using the Reynolds transport theorem and the fundamental theorem of calculus, we may rewrite this fact as

(4.16)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}^{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)}\partial_{t}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t){\,\mathrm{d}}s+\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b),t)\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)-\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),t)\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)

(4.17)

\displaystyle=\int_{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}^{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)}\left(\partial_{t}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t)+\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)\partial_{s}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t)\right){\,\mathrm{d}}s+\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),t)\partial_{t}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)-\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a))\,.

Finally, to derive (
3.6
) from this we need to compute
\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1}
. By definition (see (
2.12
)), it holds that
\mathbf{s}(X_{t}(\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s))=\sigma_{t}^{-1}(s)
, differentiating this identity in
t
and using (
3.2
) yields

(4.18)

\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(s)=\nabla\mathbf{s}(X_{t}(\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s))\cdot u_{\varepsilon}(X_{t}(\gamma_{\varepsilon,l}^{0}(s),t)=\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\,\mathbf{s}}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(s),\nu_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(s),t),t)\,.

We can then let
a-b\rightarrow 0
and see that

(4.19)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\lim_{a-b\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)-\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}\,\biggl(\int_{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}^{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)}\left(\partial_{t}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t)+\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)\partial_{s}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(s,t)\right){\,\mathrm{d}}s

(4.20)

\displaystyle\quad+\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),t)\partial_{t}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)-\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a))\biggl)

(4.21)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t)+\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\,\mathbf{s}}(\tilde{s},\nu_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t),t)\partial_{s}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t)

(4.22)

\displaystyle\quad+\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t)\lim_{a-b\rightarrow 0}\frac{\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\,\mathbf{s}}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b),\nu_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b),t),t)-\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\,\mathbf{s}}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),\nu_{\varepsilon,l}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a),t),t)}{\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)-\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)}

(4.23)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t)+\partial_{s}(\widetilde{u}_{\varepsilon}^{\,\mathbf{s}}(\tilde{s},\nu_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t),t)\varpi_{\varepsilon,l}(\tilde{s},t))),

where
\tilde{s}
denotes the common limit of
\sigma_{t}^{-1}(b)
and
\sigma_{t}^{-1}(a)
.
∎

5.
Geometric estimates

This section is devoted to proving Proposition
3.3
. We start with a preliminary technical lemma whose proof is a straightforward consequence of the fundamental theorem of calculus.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
B\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}
be convex, and let
g:B\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
be a
C^{2}
-function. Then, for all
a,b,c,d\in B
, it follows that

|g(a)-g(b)-g(c)+g(d)|\leqslant\|{\rm D}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}|a-b+d-c|+\|{\rm D}^{2}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\big(|a-b|+|c-d|\big)\big(|a-c|+|b-d|\big)\,.

We now derive the key result of this subsection, from which Proposition
3.3
will follow.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
g:\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\times B\to\mathbb{C}
be a holomorphic function, where
B\subset\mathbb{C}
is a neighborhood of
[-R_{1},R_{1}]
, with
\rho,\,R_{1}>0
fixed. Assume that
g(\mathbb{T}\times[-R_{1},R_{1}])\subset\mathbb{R}
and that
\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\times B)}<\infty
. Then, there exists
C>0
, depending only on
g
and
B
, such that, if
w^{j}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
are functions bounded as

\left\lVert w^{j}\right\rVert_{\rho}<C\,,

with
j=1,2
, then the maps
s\mapsto g(s,w^{j}(s))
are holomorphic on
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
. Moreover, they can be estimated as

(5.1)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g(\cdot,w^{j}(\cdot)))\right\rVert_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\,,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g(\cdot,w^{1}(\cdot))-g(\cdot,w^{2}(\cdot))\right\rVert_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{\rho}\,,

with implicit constants that depend only on
C
,
g
and
B
.

Remark 5.3
.

The assumptions in the previous result can be slightly relaxed. We need that

\left\lVert w^{j}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}<C_{1}\,,

for some sufficiently small
C_{1}>0
, and that the
C^{\frac{1}{2}}
-seminorm is finite, i.e.

C_{2}:=\big[w^{j}\big]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}<\infty\,.

In this case, the implicit constants would depend on both
C_{1}
and on (an upper bound of)
C_{2}
.

Proof.

We consider functions
w^{1},w^{2}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
and, using that
g
is holomorphic in
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\times B\supset\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\times[-R_{1},R_{1}]
, we get the existence of a constant
C>0
such that

\left\lVert w^{j}\right\rVert_{\rho}<C\,,

implies that the maps
s\mapsto g(s,w^{j}(s))
are holomorphic on
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. To conclude the proof, we then have to show that (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) hold. Note that, taking
B
smaller if necessary, we can assume that
\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\times B
is convex.

Let
j\in\{1,2\}
. By direct computations, we get that

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g(\cdot,w^{j})\right\rVert_{\rho}=\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\Bigg\{\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{T}}\big|g(\alpha+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha+i\beta))\big|

(5.4)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{T}\\
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\big|}{|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Bigg\}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\sup\Big\{|g(s,z)|:s\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\,\textup{ and }\,|z|\leqslant C\Big\}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\Bigg\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{T}\\
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\big|}{|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Bigg\}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim 1+\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\Bigg\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{T}\\
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\big|}{|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Bigg\}\,.

Also, observe that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\big|g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\big|

(5.9)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\|{\rm D}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\Big(\big|w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|+|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|\Big)

(5.10)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Big(\big|w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|+|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|\Big)\,.

Hence, using the control of the
\frac{1}{2}
-Hölder norm of
w^{j}
, we see that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g(\cdot,w^{j}(\cdot))\right\rVert_{\rho}\lesssim 1+\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\big[w^{j}(\cdot+i\beta)\big]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim 1+C\,,

and thus (
5.1
) follows.

Next, it follows that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g(\cdot,w^{1}(\cdot))-g(\cdot,w^{2}(\cdot))\right\rVert_{\rho}\leqslant\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{T}\\
|\beta|\leqslant\rho\end{subarray}}\big|g(\alpha+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha+i\beta))-g(\alpha+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha+i\beta))\big|

(5.13)

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\Bigg\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{T}\\
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))

(5.14)

\displaystyle\quad-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))+g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\bigg)\Bigg\}\,.

We estimate each term on the right-hand side separately. On the one hand, we have that

(5.15)

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{T}\\
|\beta|\leqslant\rho\end{subarray}}\big|g(\alpha+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha+i\beta))-g(\alpha+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha+i\beta))\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|{\rm D}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\|w^{1}(\cdot+i\beta)-w^{2}(\cdot+i\beta)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{\rho}\,.

On the other hand, taking into account Lemma
5.1
, we get that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\bigg|g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))

(5.17)

\displaystyle\quad-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))+g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\bigg|

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|{\rm D}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\Big|w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{2}\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)+w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\Big|

(5.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\|{\rm D}^{2}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\bigg(\Big(\big|w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)\big|+\big|w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)-w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|\Big)

(5.20)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\big|w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|+\big|w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|\Big)\bigg)

(5.21)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|{\rm D}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\Big|w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)+w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\Big|

(5.22)

\displaystyle\quad+2\|{\rm D}^{2}g\|_{L^{\infty}(B)}\left\lVert w^{1}(\cdot+i\beta)-w^{2}(\cdot+i\beta)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big|w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)+w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\Big|

(5.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert w^{1}(\cdot+i\beta)-w^{2}(\cdot+i\beta)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big|w^{j}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta)-w^{j}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta)\big|\,.

Hence, we have that

(5.25)

\displaystyle\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\Bigg\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{T}\\
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))-g(\alpha_{1}+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha_{1}+i\beta))

\displaystyle-g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{1}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))+g(\alpha_{2}+i\beta,w^{2}(\alpha_{2}+i\beta))\bigg)\Bigg\}\lesssim\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{\rho}\,.

Combining (
5.15
) and (
5.25
) we immediately obtain (
5.2
) and conclude the proof.
∎

The second ingredient that we need to prove Proposition
3.3
is the following lemma. Here, we use a fixed reference curve
\Gamma\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho_{0}}^{2}
for some
\rho_{0}>0
as in Theorem
2.1
with
\Gamma^{(5)}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho_{0}}^{2}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

There exists
c_{0}>0
such that
\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}
and
\kappa
are real analytic in the set
\mathbb{T}\times(-c_{0},c_{0})
. Furthermore,
|\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}
in
\mathbb{T}\times(-c_{0},c_{0})
and, for every
n\in(-c_{0},c_{0})
we have
\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(\cdot,n),\,\kappa(\cdot,n)\in{\mathcal{X}}^{2}_{\rho_{0}}
.

Proof.

First of all, recall that

\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(s,n)=\dot{\Gamma}(s)+n\ddot{\Gamma}(s)^{\perp}\quad\textup{ and }\quad\kappa(s,n):=\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\Big(\frac{\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(s,n)}{|\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(s,n)|^{2}}\Big)\,.

By assumption, it holds that
\Gamma,\Gamma^{(5)}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho_{0}}^{2}
. Hence,
\mathrm{D}^{2}\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(\cdot,n)\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho_{0}}^{2}
for all fixed
n
, and
\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}
is real analytic in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, as
\Gamma
is parametrized by arclength, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that
|\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}
in
\mathbb{T}\times(-c_{0},c_{0})
. Combining this lower bound with the definition of
\kappa
, the result follows.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

This proof also shows that
\frac{\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(\cdot,n)}{|\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}(\cdot,n)|^{2}}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho_{0}}^{2}
for all fixed
n\in(-c_{0},c_{0})
, and that
\frac{\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}}{|\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}|^{2}}
is real analytic in
\mathbb{T}\times(-c_{0},c_{0})
. Moreover, since
\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{n}}(s)=\dot{\Gamma}(s)^{\perp}
, we also have
\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho_{0}}^{2}
.

We are now ready to prove Proposition
3.3
.

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

The result follows combining Lemma
5.4
with Lemma
5.2
applied with
g=\kappa
and
g=\frac{\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}}{|\widetilde{e}_{\mathbf{s}}|^{2}}
.
∎

6.
Kernel estimates

In this section we prove Proposition
3.4
. Since the proof is rather long, we split it into several parts. We start by introducing some notation. For
z=(z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
, we denote its modulus by

|z|:=\sqrt{|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}}\in[0,\infty)\,.

Also, when
\operatorname{Re}\{z_{1}^{2}+z_{2}^{2}\}>0
(as will be the case throughout the paper), we use the main branch of the square root on
\mathbb{C}\backslash(-\infty,0)
to define

(6.1)

\displaystyle|z|_{\mathbb{C}}:=\sqrt{z_{1}^{2}+z_{2}^{2}}\in\mathbb{C}\,,

and denote the corresponding quadratic form by

(6.2)

\displaystyle\langle z,z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}:=z_{1}z_{1}^{\prime}+z_{2}z_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}\,.

Of course, (
6.1
) is not a norm. A direct calculation shows that

(6.3)

\displaystyle||z|_{\mathbb{C}}|\leqslant|z|\,.

Having this notation at hand, in the next subsection, we prove several technical lemmas which will be key to prove Proposition
3.4
.

6.1.
Preliminary lemmas

We start with a technical result whose proof is just direct calculus. We provide some details for the benefit of the reader.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
p,q,r,t\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
, then

(6.4)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{p}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p-q-r+t}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{2p\langle p,q-p+r-t\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\max\{|p|,|q|,|r|,|t|\}^{3}}{\min\{||p|_{\mathbb{C}}|,||q|_{\mathbb{C}}|,||r|_{\mathbb{C}}|,||t|_{\mathbb{C}}|\}^{6}}\big(|p-q|+|r-t|\big)\big(|p-r|+|q-t|\big)\,.

Proof.

First, note that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\frac{p}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=\frac{p-q}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r-t}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+q\frac{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-t\frac{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p-q-r+t}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-(r-t)\frac{\langle p-r,p+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+q\frac{\langle q-p,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-t\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,.

Now, observe that

\displaystyle t\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=q\frac{\langle t-r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+q\langle t-r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+q\frac{\langle t-r,t-q+r-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+(t-q)\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\,,

and so that

(6.6)

\displaystyle q\frac{\langle q-p,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-t\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=q\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.7)

\displaystyle\qquad-q\langle t-r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)-q\frac{\langle t-r,t-q+r-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+(q-t)\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\,.

On the other hand, it follows that

(6.8)

\displaystyle q\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=2q\frac{\langle q-t-p+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+q\frac{\langle q-t-p+r,q-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}

(6.9)

\displaystyle\quad=2p\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}+2p\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\langle p-q,p+q\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.10)

\displaystyle\qquad+q\frac{\langle q-t-p+r,q-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+2(q-p)\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\,.

Hence, we actually have that

(6.11)

\displaystyle q\frac{\langle q-p,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-t\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=2p\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}+2p\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\langle p-q,p+q\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\qquad+q\frac{\langle q-t-p+r,q-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+2(q-p)\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}

(6.13)

\displaystyle\qquad-q\langle t-r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)-q\frac{\langle t-r,t-q+r-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+(q-t)\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\,.

Finally, we substitute everything into (
6.5
) and get that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\frac{p}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p-q-r+t}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{2p\langle p,q-p+r-t\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

(6.15)

\displaystyle\quad=(t-r)\frac{\langle p-r,p+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+2p\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\langle p-q,p+q\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.16)

\displaystyle\qquad+q\frac{\langle q-t-p+r,q-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+2(q-p)\frac{\langle q-p-t+r,p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}

(6.17)

\displaystyle\qquad-q\langle t-r,q+p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)-q\frac{\langle t-r,t-q+r-p\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+(q-t)\frac{\langle t-r,t+r\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\,.

Then, using (
6.3
), one can estimate each term on the right hand side and conclude the proof.
∎

In the next result, using classical harmonic analysis tools, we estimate some singular integrals in
C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})
. We do it for a general Calderón–Zygmund kernel depending on two complex parameters
a,b\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Here,
\phi
is the periodic extension to
\mathbb{T}
of a smooth even cutoff function such that

\phi(s)=1\quad\textup{if }|s|\leqslant\frac{1}{8}\,,\quad\textup{ and }\quad\phi(s)=0\quad\textup{if }|s|\geqslant\frac{1}{4}\,.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
a,b\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\backslash\{0\}
be fixed and such that, for some
A_{0}\geqslant 1
,

(6.18)

\displaystyle||a|_{\mathbb{C}}-1|\leqslant\frac{1}{10}

\displaystyle\mathrm{and}\quad||b|_{\mathbb{C}}-||b|_{\mathbb{C}}||\leqslant\frac{1}{4}||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\,,

\displaystyle|a|\leqslant A_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrm{and}\quad||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\geqslant\frac{1}{10A_{0}}|b|\,,

and

(6.19)

\displaystyle|\langle a,b\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|\leqslant\frac{1}{100A_{0}}|b|\,.

Consider convolution kernels
f:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{C}
defined for
|x|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}
by

(6.20)

\displaystyle f(x):=\frac{x^{m}\varepsilon^{r}}{|xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{r+m+1}}\phi(x)\,\mathds{1}_{c\leqslant|x|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}}\,,

with
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
c\in[0,1/2]
,
r,m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, and
r\neq 0
or
m
odd. Then,

(6.21)

\left\lVert f*g\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|b|^{-r}\,\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for all }g\in C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})\,.

Moreover, for
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, the derivatives of f with respect to the parameters a and b satisfy

(6.22)

\left\lVert({\rm D}^{k_{1}}_{a}{\rm D}_{b}^{k_{2}}f)*g\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|b|^{-r-k_{2}}\,\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for all }g\in C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})\,.

The implicit constants here depend only on
k_{1},k_{2},r,m
and
A_{0}
.

Proof.

First of all, combining (
6.18
) and (
6.19
) with Young’s inequality, we get that

(6.23)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}|ax+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\left\{x^{2}|a|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+2\varepsilon x\langle a,b\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geqslant x^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|^{2}-x^{2}(||a|_{\mathbb{C}}-1|^{2}+2||a|_{\mathbb{C}}-1|)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}(||b|_{\mathbb{C}}-||b|_{\mathbb{C}}||^{2}+2||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|||b|_{\mathbb{C}}-||b|_{\mathbb{C}}||)-2\varepsilon|x||\langle a,b\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|\gtrsim x^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|b|^{2}\,.

Having this lower bound at hand, we analyze separately the cases
r>0
and
r=0
.

Case 1:
r>0
. Note that (
6.23
) implies

(6.24)

|f(x)|\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon^{r}|x|^{m}}{|x|^{r+m+1}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{r+m+1}}\,,\quad\textup{for all }|x|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\textup{ and all }r\geqslant 0\,.

Hence, since we are dealing with the case where
r>0
, we get that

(6.25)

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}|f(x)|{\,\mathrm{d}}x\lesssim\varepsilon^{r}\int_{-\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{|x|^{m}}{|x|^{r+m+1}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{r+m+1}}{\,\mathrm{d}}x

(6.26)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{\varepsilon|b|^{r+1}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon|b|}\big)^{m}}{1+\big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon|b|}\big)^{r+m+1}}{\,\mathrm{d}}x\leqslant\frac{2}{|b|^{r}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{m}}{1+\rho^{r+m+1}}d\rho\lesssim|b|^{-r}\,.

In particular, the convolution
f*g
is always well-defined for
g\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})
. Furthermore, using Young’s convolution inequality, we get that

(6.27)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g*f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|b|^{-r}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,

(6.28)

\displaystyle\left\lVert g*f(\cdot)-g*f(\cdot+y)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|b|^{-r}\left\lVert g(\cdot)-g(\cdot+y)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|b|^{-r}|y|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})},

which shows (
6.21
) in the case where
r>0
.

Case 2:
r=0
.
In this case, the convolution needs to be estimated as a singular integral.
By standard Fourier multiplier theorems (see for instance
[
23
, Corollary 6.7.2 and Remark 6.5.2]
and
[
22
, Theorem 4.4.1]
) it suffices to show that

(6.29)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<R\leqslant\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{R}\int_{-R}^{R}|f(x)||x|\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\,,

(6.30)

\displaystyle\sup_{y\neq 0}\int_{|x|\geqslant 2|y|}|f(x-y)-f(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\,,

(6.31)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<R_{1}<R_{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}}\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim 1\,.

The first condition (
6.29
) immediately follows from (
6.24
) arguing as in the case where
r>0
. Hence, we focus on the other two conditions.

We first deal with (
6.30
). Here, we distinguish the cases
|y|\geqslant c
and
|y|\leqslant c
. Note that in the first case

(6.32)

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geqslant 2|y|}|f(x-y)-f(x)|{\,\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{|x|\geqslant 2|y|}\left|\int_{-1}^{0}f^{\prime}(x+\tau y)y{\,\mathrm{d}}\tau\right|{\,\mathrm{d}}x\,.

Thus, using that

|x+\tau y|\geqslant|x|-|y|\geqslant|y|\,,\quad\textup{for all }x,y\in[-1/2,1/2]\textup{ with }|x|\geqslant 2|y|\,,

we get that

(6.33)

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geqslant 2|y|}|f(x-y)-f(x)|{\,\mathrm{d}}x\leqslant\int_{|z|\geqslant|y|}|y||f^{\prime}(z)|{\,\mathrm{d}}z\,.

On the other hand, combining again (
6.23
) with (
6.18
), we get that

|f^{\prime}(z)|\lesssim\frac{|z|^{m-1}}{|z|^{m+1}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{m+1}}+\frac{|z|^{m}}{|z|^{m+1}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{m+1}}+\frac{|z|^{m}}{|z|^{m+2}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{m+2}}\,,

and so that

(6.34)

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geqslant 2|y|}|f(x-y)-f(x)|{\,\mathrm{d}}x\lesssim|y|\int_{|y|}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}{\,\mathrm{d}}\rho\lesssim 1\,.

This immediately gives (
6.30
) in the case where
|y|\geqslant c
.

In the other case, i.e. when
|y|\leqslant c
, we treat separately the cases where
|x|\geqslant 2c
and
|x|\leqslant 2c
. Note that here we are implicitly assuming that
c>0
. If
|x|\leqslant 2c
, then
|x-y|\leqslant 3c
and hence, using (
6.24
), we can estimate

(6.35)

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geqslant 2|y|}|f(x-y)-f(x)|{\,\mathrm{d}}x\leqslant 2\int_{|x|\in[c,3c]}|f(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{c}^{3c}\frac{1}{x}{\,\mathrm{d}}x\lesssim\log(3c)-\log(c)\lesssim 1\,,

showing (
6.30
). On the other hand, if
|x|\geqslant 2c
, we have that

|x+\tau y|\geqslant|x|-|y|\geqslant c\,,

and we can argue exactly as we did when
|y|\geqslant c
and obtain again (
6.30
).

We finally deal with (
6.31
). Let
0<R_{1}<R_{2}\leqslant 1/2
. We need to consider three different cases separately.

Case 2.1:
R_{2}\leqslant 10\varepsilon|b|
.
In this case, using once again (
6.24
), we immediately get that

(6.36)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leqslant\int_{-10\varepsilon|b|}^{10\varepsilon|b|}|f(x)|dx\lesssim\int_{-10}^{10}\frac{\rho^{m}}{1+\rho^{m+1}}{\,\mathrm{d}}\rho\lesssim 1\,.

Case 2.2:
R_{1}\geqslant 10\varepsilon|b|
.
Since
m
is odd, it follows that

\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|=\left|\int_{R_{1}}^{R_{2}}(f(x)+f(-x)){\,\mathrm{d}}x\right|\leqslant\int_{R_{1}}^{R_{2}}x^{m}\left|\frac{1}{|xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{m+1}}-\frac{1}{|-xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{m+1}}\right|{\,\mathrm{d}}x\,.

Moreover, using (
6.19
), (
6.23
), and the polarization identity

||xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|-xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|=4|\operatorname{Re}\langle ax,\varepsilon b\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|\,,

we get that

\left|\frac{1}{|xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{m+1}}-\frac{1}{|-xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{m+1}}\right|\lesssim\frac{|\operatorname{Re}\langle ax,\varepsilon b\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|}{x^{m+3}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{m+3}}\lesssim\frac{x\varepsilon|b|}{x^{m+3}+(\varepsilon|b|)^{m+3}}\,,\quad\textup{for }x\in[R_{1},R_{2}]\,.

Thus, we infer that

\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon|b|}\int_{10\varepsilon|b|}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon|b|}\big)^{m+1}}{1+\big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon|b|}\big)^{m+3}}\,{\,\mathrm{d}}x\lesssim 1\,.

Case 2.3:
R_{1}<10\varepsilon|b|<R_{2}
.
Note that, in this case

\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leqslant\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant 10\varepsilon|b|}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|+\left|\int_{10\varepsilon|b|\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\,.

Hence, combining the previous cases, we get that

\left|\int_{R_{1}\leqslant|x|\leqslant R_{2}}f(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\lesssim 1\,.

We have now proved (
6.29
)–(
6.31
). Hence, by
[
23
, Corollary 6.7.2 and Remark 6.5.2]
and
[
22
, Theorem 4.4.1]
, we conclude that (
6.21
) holds.

Concerning (
6.22
), we simply note that the derivatives of
f
with respect to
a_{j}
and
b_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, are a linear combination of terms with the same structure as
f
, the only difference being that each derivative with respect to
b_{j}
introduces a factor
\varepsilon
. Each additional
\varepsilon
can be absorbed in the estimates by picking an additional factor
|b|
, with the same argument as above. This is the content of the estimate (
6.22
).
∎

6.2.
First steps towards the proof of Proposition
3.4

We consider the operator
K_{\varepsilon}
introduced in (
3.14
) and point out that one can equivalently write this operator as

K_{\varepsilon}[\nu_{1},\nu_{2},g,\varpi](s+i\beta)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{d^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)^{\perp}}{\big|d^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,\varpi(\varsigma+i\beta){\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\,,

where

(6.37)

\displaystyle d^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\digamma}(s+i\beta)-\tilde{\digamma}(\varsigma+i\beta)+\varepsilon\tilde{\zeta}(s+i\beta)\,,

with

(6.38)

\displaystyle\tilde{\digamma}(a+ib)

\displaystyle:=\Gamma(a+ib)+\nu_{1}(a+ib)\,\dot{\Gamma}(a+ib)^{\perp}\,,

(6.39)

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}(a+ib)

\displaystyle:=-g(a+ib)\dot{\Gamma}(a+ib)^{\perp}\,.

Moreover, for later purposes, and with some abuse of notation, we set

(6.40)

\displaystyle d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)=\Gamma(s+i\beta)-\Gamma(\varsigma+i\beta)+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}-w_{1}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(\varsigma+i\beta)^{\perp}

(6.41)

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}\big(1+w_{3}[\mu,s+i\beta]\big){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}=\digamma(s+i\beta)-\digamma(\varsigma+i\beta)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta)\,,

with

(6.42)

\digamma(a+ib):=\Gamma(a+ib)+w_{1}[\ell,a+ib]\,\dot{\Gamma}(a+ib)^{\perp}\,,

and

(6.43)

\zeta(a+ib):=-\bigg(\int_{l}^{\ell}\big(1+w_{3}[\mu,a+ib]\big){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigg)\dot{\Gamma}(a+ib)^{\perp}\,.

Then, we have that

(6.44)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,w_{2}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s+i\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\,,

Remark 6.3
.

Both
\digamma
and
\zeta
depend on
w
,
l
and
\ell
, but we do not reflect this in the notation for convenience. Moreover, taking into account the notation introduced in Section
3
, one can notice that
\digamma=\gamma_{\varepsilon,\ell}
when restricted to
\mathbb{T}
.

Having this notation at hand, and before going any further, let us stress that through the rest of this section, we consider
0\leqslant\rho<\rho_{0}
and
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfying

(6.45)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}w_{1}[l,\cdot]=w_{2}[l,\cdot]\,,\quad\textup{for all }|l|\leqslant 1\,,

(6.46)

\displaystyle w_{1}[l,\cdot]-w_{1}[\ell,\cdot]=\varepsilon\int_{\ell}^{l}\big(1+w_{3}[\mu,\cdot]\big){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\,,\quad\textup{for all }l,\ell\in[-1,1]\,.

Moreover, we assume in all the proofs that
l\neq\ell
(which is not restrictive as
\{l-\ell\}
is a zero set). Taking into account (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), it is straightforward to check that

(6.47)

\displaystyle\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\Big\{\left\lVert\digamma(\cdot+i\beta)\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}+\frac{1}{|l-\ell|}\left\lVert\zeta(\cdot+i\beta)\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}+\varepsilon\left\lVert\zeta(\cdot+i\beta)\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{j}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho},

where we recall that
\digamma
and
\zeta
were given in (
6.42
) and (
6.43
) respectively. Also, we set

\tilde{d}(v,s+i\beta):=v\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta)\,,

where there derivative refers to the derivative in
s
, and split the kernel in (
6.44
) as

(6.48)

\displaystyle\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=J_{1}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)+J_{2}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)+J_{3}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\,,

with

(6.49)

\displaystyle J_{1}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta):=\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\phi(s-\varsigma)\,,

(6.50)

\displaystyle J_{2}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta):=\left(\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\phi(s-\varsigma)\,,

(6.51)

\displaystyle J_{3}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta):=\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}(1-\phi(s-\varsigma))\,.

Here,
\phi
is the periodic extension to
\mathbb{T}
of a smooth even cutoff function such that

\phi(s)=1\quad\textup{if }|s|\leqslant\frac{1}{8}\,,\quad\textup{ and }\quad\phi(s)=0\quad\textup{if }|s|\geqslant\frac{1}{4}\,.

6.3.
Proof of (
3.27
)

This subsection is devoted to prove (
3.27
). Having (
6.48
) at hand, we analyze the part corresponding to each kernel
J_{j}
separately. We start with the one corresponding to
J_{1}
. We treat it as a convolution operator on
\mathbb{T}
with an extra dependence on
s+i\beta\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}\,.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
a=a(s+i\beta):=\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
and
b=b(s+i\beta):=\zeta(s+i\beta)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
. There exists a constant
C_{1}>0
depending only on
\Gamma
such that, if
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
\mathcal{C}_{2}\leqslant C_{1}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then
a
and
b
satisfy (
6.18
) and (
6.19
) for some
A_{0}\geqslant 1
depending only on
\Gamma
.

Proof.

First of all, it directly follows from (
6.47
) that
||a|_{\mathbb{C}}|\leqslant|a|\lesssim 1
. Moreover, as
\Gamma
is parametrized by arclenght, we have that

(6.52)

|\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}=1\,,

and so that

(6.53)

\displaystyle||a|_{\mathbb{C}}-1|=\Big|\big|\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)+w_{2}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\ddot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}\big|_{\mathbb{C}}-\big|\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}\Big|\,.

Now, observe that

(6.54)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\big|\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)+w_{2}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\ddot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}\big|_{\mathbb{C}}

(6.55)

\displaystyle\qquad\geqslant 1-C\big(\|w_{1}[\ell,\cdot]\|_{\rho}+\|w_{2}[\ell,\cdot]\|_{\rho}\big)\,,

for some
C>0
depending only on
\Gamma
. Hence, there exists
C_{1}>0
sufficiently small such that, if (
3.26
) holds with
\mathcal{C}_{2}\leqslant C_{1}
, then

\operatorname{Re}\big|\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)+w_{2}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\ddot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}\big|_{\mathbb{C}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\,.

On the other hand, observe that
|\cdot|_{\mathbb{C}}
is locally Lipschitz away from the set of points
z\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
where
|z|_{\mathbb{C}}=0
. Hence, we have that

(6.56)

||a|_{\mathbb{C}}-1|\lesssim\left|w_{2}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\ddot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}\right|\lesssim\|w_{1}[\ell,\cdot]\|_{\rho}+\|w_{2}[\ell,\cdot]\|_{\rho}\,.

Note that all the implicit constants here depend only on
\Gamma
. Taking
C_{1}>0
smaller if necessary we conclude that

||a|_{\mathbb{C}}-1|\leqslant\frac{1}{10}\,.

Next, observe that

(6.57)

\displaystyle\left||b|_{\mathbb{C}}-||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{4}||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\ \Longleftrightarrow\ ||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|-\operatorname{Re}|b|_{\mathbb{C}}\leqslant\frac{1}{32}||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\Longleftrightarrow\ \operatorname{Re}|b|_{\mathbb{C}}\geqslant\frac{31}{32}||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\,.

Moreover, by (
6.52
), it follows that

|b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}=(l-\ell)^{2}\left(1+\fint_{l}^{\ell}w_{3}[\mu,s+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)^{2}\,,

and so that

(6.58)

\operatorname{Re}|b|_{\mathbb{C}}\geqslant|l-\ell|\Big(1-\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{3}[l,s+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\Big)\,,

and that

||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\leqslant|l-\ell|\Big(1+\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{3}[l,s+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\Big)\,.

Hence, to have that

(6.59)

||b|_{\mathbb{C}}-||b|_{\mathbb{C}}||\leqslant\frac{1}{4}||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\,,

it is enough to impose that

\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{3}[l,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leqslant\frac{1}{64}\,.

Taking
C_{1}>0
smaller if necessary we conclude that (
6.59
) holds. On the other hand,

|b|\leqslant|l-\ell|(1+\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\|w_{3}[l,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})})\|\dot{\Gamma}(\cdot+i\beta)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|l-\ell|(1+\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\|w_{3}[l,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})})\,,

for some implicit constant depending only on
\Gamma
. Using (
6.58
), it is then straightforward to obtain the existence of
A_{0}\geqslant 1
depending only on
C_{1}>0
(and thus on
\Gamma
) such that

||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|\geqslant\frac{1}{10A_{0}}|b|\,.

For later purposes, let us point out that taking
C_{1}>0
smaller would not affect the existence of
A_{0}\geqslant 1
. This constant actually depends on an upper bound of
C_{1}>0
. This concludes the proof of (
6.18
).

We now prove (
6.19
). To that end, let us first stress that

\langle\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp},\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}=0\,.

Hence, we have that

(6.60)

\displaystyle|\langle a,b\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\bigg\langle w_{2}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}

(6.61)

\displaystyle\qquad+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\ddot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp},\bigg(\int_{l}^{\ell}\big(1+w_{3}[\mu,a+ib]\big){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigg)\dot{\Gamma}(\varsigma+i\beta)^{\perp}\bigg\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\bigg|

(6.62)

\displaystyle\leqslant||w_{2}[\ell,s+i\beta]\dot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}+w_{1}[\ell,s+i\beta]\ddot{\Gamma}(s+i\beta)^{\perp}|_{\mathbb{C}}|\,||b|_{\mathbb{C}}|

(6.63)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|w_{1}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\|w_{2}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\big)|b|\,,

for some implicit constant depending only on
\Gamma
. Taking
C_{1}>0
smaller if necessary we get (
6.19
) and conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
C_{1}>0
be as in Lemma
6.4
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
\mathcal{C}_{2}\leqslant C_{1}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{1}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}\lesssim\,\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}\,.

Proof.

For
a
and
b
as in Lemma
6.4
, observe that

J_{1}(x,s+i\beta)=a^{\perp}\frac{x}{|xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+b^{\perp}\frac{\varepsilon}{|xa+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,.

By Lemma
6.2
, applied first with
m=1
and
r=0
and then with
m=0
and
r=1
, we get that

(6.64)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{1}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Now, observe that

(6.65)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{1}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]-J_{1}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]\big){\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

(6.66)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{1}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma-\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{1}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

(6.67)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(J_{1}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta)-J_{1}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta)\right)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|=:{\rm I}_{1}+{\rm I}_{2}\,.

Arguing as in the proof of (
6.64
), using Lemma
6.2
twice, we get that

(6.68)

{\rm I}_{1}\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

To analyze
{\rm I}_{2}
, using the notation
a_{j}:=a(s_{j}+i\beta)
and
b_{j}:=b(s_{j}+i\beta)
for
a
and
b
as in Lemma
6.4
, we split the convolution kernel as

(6.69)

\displaystyle J_{1}(x,s_{1}+i\beta)-J_{1}(x,s_{2}+i\beta)=(a_{1}-a_{2})^{\perp}\frac{x}{|xa_{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+(b_{1}-b_{2})^{\perp}\frac{\varepsilon}{|xa_{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.70)

\displaystyle\ +(xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{2})^{\perp}\left(\frac{1}{|xa_{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{|xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)+(xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{2})^{\perp}\left(\frac{1}{|xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{|xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\,.

Then, we observe that, by (
6.47
),

(6.71)

|a_{1}-a_{2}|=|\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)|\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

and

|b_{1}-b_{2}|=|\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta)-\zeta(s_{2}+i\beta)|\lesssim|l-\ell||s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Also, by (
6.3
) and (
6.58
), it follows that

(6.72)

|b_{j}|\geqslant||b_{j}|_{\mathbb{C}}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}|l-\ell|\,,\quad\textup{for }j\in\{1,2\}\,.

Combining the decomposition of the kernel above with these estimates and Lemma
6.2
, we conclude that

(6.73)

{\rm I}_{2}\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

The result follows combining (
6.64
), (
6.68
) and (
6.73
).
∎

We now move on to the analysis of the part corresponding to
J_{2}
. A key role in this analysis is played by Lemma
6.1
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
C_{1}>0
be as in Lemma
6.4
. There exists a constant
C_{2}\in(0,C_{1}]
depending only on
\Gamma
such that, if
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
\mathcal{C}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then, for
s+i\beta\in\mathbb{T}_{\rho}
and
\varsigma\in\mathbb{T}
:

(6.74)

\displaystyle\textup{\rm a) }\ |d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|+|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|\lesssim|s-\varsigma|+\varepsilon|l-\ell|\,,

(6.75)

\displaystyle\textup{\rm b) }\ |d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)-\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\,,

(6.76)

\displaystyle\textup{\rm c) }\ \min\Big\{\operatorname{Re}|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,,\,\operatorname{Re}|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\Big\}\gtrsim|s-\varsigma|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|l-\ell|^{2}\,.

Proof.

First, let us recall that

d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)=\digamma(s+i\beta)-\digamma(\varsigma+i\beta)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta)\,,

and that

\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)=(s-\varsigma)\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta)\,.

We prove each estimate separately. Since the upper bounds in (
6.74
) are a straightforward consequence of (
6.47
), we focus on b) and c). Using again (
6.47
) and the mean value theorem, it is also immediate that

(6.77)

\displaystyle|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)-\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|

(6.78)

\displaystyle=\left|\digamma(s+i\beta)-\digamma(\varsigma+i\beta)-(s-\varsigma)\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta)\right|\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left\lVert\digamma(\cdot+i\beta)\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\,.

Finally, we prove the lower bounds in c). For
a
and
b
as in Lemma
6.4
, it follows that

|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}=|(s-\varsigma)a+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\,.

Hence, if (
3.26
) holds with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{1}
for
C_{1}>0
as in Lemma
6.4
, combining (
6.23
) and (
6.72
), we get that

\operatorname{Re}|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\gtrsim|s-\varsigma|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|l-\ell|^{2}\,.

Next, using (
6.3
), (
6.74
) and (
6.75
), we get that, whenever
|s-\varsigma|\leqslant 1
,

(6.79)

\displaystyle||\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|

(6.80)

\displaystyle\quad=|\langle\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)-d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta),\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)+d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|

(6.81)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)-d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|^{2}

(6.82)

\displaystyle\qquad+2|\langle\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)-d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta),d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|

(6.83)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)-d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|^{2}

(6.84)

\displaystyle\qquad+2|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)||\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)-d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|

(6.85)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(|s-\varsigma|^{2}+\varepsilon|l-\ell||s-\varsigma|\big)

(6.86)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(|s-\varsigma|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|l-\ell|^{2}\big)\,.

Also, observe that

(6.87)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}=\operatorname{Re}\big\{|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\big\}

(6.88)

\displaystyle\quad\geqslant\operatorname{Re}|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-||\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|\,.

Hence, there exists
c\in(0,1)
depending only on
\Gamma
such that, if
|s-\varsigma|\leqslant c
, then

\operatorname{Re}|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\gtrsim|s-\varsigma|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|l-\ell|^{2}\,.

We now deal with the case where
|s-\varsigma|\geqslant c
. In this case, by the assumption (
2.2
), it holds that

(6.89)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}|\Gamma(s+i\beta)-\Gamma(\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\geqslant\sigma,

for some
\sigma\in(0,1)
, independent of
\varepsilon
.
Hence, if we pick
C_{2}\in(0,C_{1}]
sufficiently small and assume that
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, we see from the triangle inequality and the definition of
d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}
that

(6.90)

\operatorname{Re}|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\sigma\gtrsim|s-\varsigma|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|l-\ell|^{2}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Having this lemma at hand, and before going any further, let us point out that the fact that
K_{\varepsilon}
is holomorphic for
l\neq\ell
is a straightforward consequence of the lower bound in c). More precisely, we have the following:

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, all
l\neq\ell
, and every
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}_{\rho}^{4}
satisfying (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), it follows that

K_{\varepsilon}\bigg[w_{1}[\ell,\cdot],w_{2}[\ell,\cdot],\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\bigg]\in{\mathcal{Y}}_{\rho}^{2}\,.

Proof.

First of all, let us recall that

(6.91)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\bigg[w_{1}[\ell,\cdot],w_{2}[\ell,\cdot],\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\bigg](s+i\beta)

(6.92)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{(\digamma(s+i\beta)-\digamma(s+i\beta+\varsigma)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta))^{\perp}}{|\digamma(s+i\beta)-\digamma(s+i\beta+\varsigma)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}w_{4}[\ell,s+i\beta+\varsigma]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma

Moreover, by Lemma
6.6
c) we know that

\operatorname{Re}|\digamma(s+i\beta)-\digamma(s+i\beta+\varsigma)+\varepsilon\zeta(s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\gtrsim\varsigma^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}|l-\ell|^{2}\,.

Hence, since
l\neq\ell
, the result immediately follows.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{2}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}\lesssim\,\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}\,.

Proof.

First of all, observe that

(6.93)

\displaystyle|J_{2}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta))^{\perp}}{|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{(\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta))^{\perp}}{|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\phi(s-\varsigma)

(6.94)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)-\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)\big)|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}{|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\phi(s-\varsigma)

(6.95)

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\frac{\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)\big(|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\big)}{|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta)|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\phi(s-\varsigma)\,.

From Lemma
6.6
and the elementary formula
||p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}-|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|\leqslant|p-q||p+q|
we hence see that

(6.96)

\displaystyle\left|J_{2}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\right|\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathds{1}_{|s-\varsigma|\leqslant\frac{1}{4}}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

and so that

(6.97)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{2}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\int_{|s-\varsigma|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}}\frac{{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma}{|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Now, to bound the corresponding Hölder seminorm, we use that

(6.98)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{2}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)-J_{2}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

(6.99)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\leqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\big(|J_{2}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|+|J_{2}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)|\big)\left|w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]\right|{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma

(6.100)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\left(J_{2}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)-J_{2}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\right)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|=:{\rm I}_{1}+{\rm I}_{2}\,.

On one hand, using (
6.96
) and arguing as in (
6.97
), we get that that

(6.101)

{\rm I}_{1}\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

On the other hand, to estimate
{\rm I}_{2}
, we consider separately the cases where
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\geqslant\frac{1}{32}
and where
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leqslant\frac{1}{32}
.

Case 1:

|s_{1}-s_{2}|\geqslant\frac{1}{32}
. In this case, using again (
6.96
), it is immediate to get that

(6.102)

{\rm I}_{2}\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Case 2:

|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leqslant\frac{1}{32}
. First of all, we set

(6.103)

\displaystyle p=p(\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta):=\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta)\,,

(6.104)

\displaystyle q=q(\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta):=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\,,

(6.105)

\displaystyle r=r(\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta):=\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta)\,,

(6.106)

\displaystyle t=t(\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta):=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\,,

and split the kernel in
{\rm I}_{2}
as

(6.107)

\displaystyle J_{2}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)-J_{2}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)=\left(\frac{p^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q^{\perp}}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)

(6.108)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\big(\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big)\,.

Then, using the mean value theorem, we get that

(6.109)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\big|\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big|=\left|\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\left|\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(\lambda(s_{1}-s_{2})+s_{2}-\varsigma){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\right||s_{1}-s_{2}|\,.

Moreover, observe that

(6.110)

\phi^{\prime}(\lambda(s_{1}-s_{2})+s_{2}-\varsigma)\neq 0\ \Longrightarrow\ |s_{1}-\varsigma|\geqslant\frac{1}{16}\ \Longrightarrow\ |s_{2}-\varsigma|\geqslant\frac{1}{32}\,.

Hence, by (
6.76
), we get that

\left|\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\big|\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big|\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

and so that

(6.111)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\left(\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\big(\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

To estimate the remaining part, we are going to use Lemma
6.1
. First of all, observe that

(6.112)

\displaystyle\frac{p-q-r+t}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=-\frac{\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.113)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\big(\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\big)\,.

On one hand, combining (
6.47
) with (
6.76
) (see also the proof of (
6.75
)), we get that

(6.114)

\left|\frac{\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\lesssim\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|s_{1}-\varsigma|^{2}}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

and so that

(6.115)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{1}{|s_{1}-\varsigma|^{2}}{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

On the other hand, using Lemma
6.2
with
c=3|s_{1}-s_{2}|
,
r=0
and
m=1
, Lemma
6.4
, and (
6.71
), we get that

(6.116)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)\big(\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\big)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)\mathds{1}_{|s_{1}-\varsigma|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\,\big(\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\big)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Hence, we have that

(6.117)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{p^{\perp}-q^{\perp}-r^{\perp}+t^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Next, observe that

(6.118)

\displaystyle\frac{2p\langle p,p-q+r-t\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}=\frac{2p\langle p,\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

(6.119)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2p\langle p,(s_{1}-\varsigma)(\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta))\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\,.

Moreover, using (
6.3
), it is immediate to check that

(6.120)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2p\langle p,\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\right|

(6.121)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left|\frac{\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Thus, by (
6.114
) and (
6.115
), it follows that

(6.122)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{2p\langle p,\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)-(s_{1}-s_{2})\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\,w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

On the other hand, letting
p_{1},p_{2}
and
\digamma_{1},\digamma_{2}
denote the components of
p
and
\digamma
respectively and expanding the scalar product, we observe that

(6.123)

\displaystyle\frac{p\langle p,(s_{1}-\varsigma)(\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta))\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

(6.124)

\displaystyle=\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)p_{1}p_{2}\big((\digamma^{\prime}_{2},\digamma^{\prime}_{1})(s_{1}+i\beta)-((\digamma^{\prime}_{2},\digamma^{\prime}_{1})(s_{2}+i\beta)\big)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

(6.125)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)\left(p_{1}^{2}(\digamma_{1}^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma_{1}^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)),\ p_{2}^{2}(\digamma_{2}^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma_{2}^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta))\right)}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\,.

We then observe that each of the corresponding parts of the kernel has the structure in Lemma
6.2
by Lemma
6.4
. Indeed it holds that

(6.126)

\displaystyle(s_{1}-\varsigma)\frac{p_{1}^{2}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}=\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{3}a_{1}^{2}+2\varepsilon(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{2}a_{1}b_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma)b_{1}^{2}}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

(6.127)

\displaystyle(s_{1}-\varsigma)\frac{p_{1}p_{2}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}=\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{3}a_{1}a_{2}+\varepsilon(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{2}(a_{2}b_{1}+a_{1}b_{2})+\varepsilon^{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma)b_{1}b_{2}}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

(6.128)

\displaystyle(s_{1}-\varsigma)\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}=\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{3}a_{2}^{2}+2\varepsilon(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{2}a_{2}b_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma)b_{2}^{2}}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a+\varepsilon b|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\,,

where
a_{1},a_{2},b_{1},b_{2}
are the components of
a
and
b
in Lemma
6.4
with
s=s_{1}
. Taking into account (
6.47
) and (
6.72
), we then conclude from Lemma
6.2
that

(6.129)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{2p\langle p,(s_{1}-\varsigma)(\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta))\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

and so that

(6.130)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{2p^{\perp}\langle p^{\perp},p^{\perp}-q^{\perp}+r^{\perp}-t^{\perp}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\,\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Finally, we estimate the right-hand side in Lemma
6.1
. First, observe that, by (
6.75
), whenever
|s_{1}-\varsigma|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|
, it follows that

(6.131)

\displaystyle|r-t|+|p-q|\lesssim|s_{1}-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Likewise, using (
6.47
), we get that

(6.132)

\displaystyle|p-r|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|(s_{1}-\varsigma)\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta)-(s_{2}-\varsigma)\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta)\right|+\varepsilon\left|\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta)-\zeta(s_{2}+i\beta)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-\varsigma||s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|s_{1}-s_{2}|\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

and that, for
|\beta|\leqslant\rho
,

(6.133)

\displaystyle|q-t|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\digamma(s_{1}+i\beta)-\digamma(s_{2}+i\beta)\right|+\varepsilon\left|\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta)-\zeta(s_{2}+i\beta)\right|\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|\,,

Then, using the shortened notation

(6.134)

{\rm K}(p,q,r,t):=\frac{\max\{|p|,|q|,|r|,|t|\}^{3}}{\min\{||p|_{\mathbb{C}}|,||q|_{\mathbb{C}}|,||r|_{\mathbb{C}}|,||t|_{\mathbb{C}}|\}^{6}}\big(|p-q|+|r-t|\big)\big(|p-r|+|q-t|\big)\,,

we infer from (
6.76
),(
6.74
), (
6.131
), (
6.132
) and (
6.133
) that

(6.135)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|s_{1}-\varsigma|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{\rm K}(p,q,r,t)\,\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,\int_{3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}^{1}x^{-\frac{3}{2}}(x|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|s_{1}-s_{2}|)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Combining (
6.117
), (
6.130
), (
6.135
) and Lemma
6.1
, we get that

(6.136)

{\rm I}_{2}\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

The result follows from (
6.97
), (
6.101
), (
6.102
) and (
6.136
).
∎

Finally, concerning
J_{3}
, we have the following:

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}J_{3}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}\lesssim\,\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}\,.

Proof.

Note that
J_{3}
is nonzero only whenever
|s-\varsigma|\geqslant\frac{1}{8}
. Thus, by (
6.76
), we have that

\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\gtrsim 1\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

in its support. The result then follows from the definition of
d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta)
and (
6.47
).
∎

Having the previous lemmas at hand, we can now conclude.

Proof of (
3.27
).

Taking into account Lemma
6.7
and (
6.48
), (
3.27
) immediately follows from Lemmas
6.5
,
6.8
and
6.9
.
∎

6.4.
Proof of (
3.28
)

In this subsection we prove (
3.28
). Since the proof is similar to the one of (
3.27
), we will skip some details. First of all, taking into account (
6.44
), we see that

(6.137)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,w_{2}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{1}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{1}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s+i\beta)

\displaystyle-K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{2}[\ell,\cdot],\,w_{2}^{2}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{2}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s+i\beta)

\displaystyle=K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,w_{2}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{1}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{1}[\ell,\cdot]-w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s+i\beta)

\displaystyle+K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,w_{2}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{1}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s+i\beta)

\displaystyle-K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{2}[\ell,\cdot],\,w_{2}^{2}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{2}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s+i\beta)\,.

The first term on the right hand side can be directly estimated using (
3.27
). Indeed, we have:

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\lVert K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{1}[l,\cdot],\,w_{1}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{1}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{1}[\ell,\cdot]-w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right](\cdot)\right\rVert_{\rho}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}^{1}[l,\cdot]-w_{4}^{2}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}.

We now focus on the other two terms, and analyze them together. First, we prove the following:

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}\bigg\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\bigg\|

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{1}[l,\cdot],\,w_{1}^{1}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{1}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right]

\displaystyle-K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}^{2}[l,\cdot],\,w_{1}^{2}[\ell,\cdot],\,\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{2}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,\,w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\cdot]\right]\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\bigg\}\lesssim\,\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Proof.

Taking into account (
6.48
), we analyze the part corresponding to each kernel
J_{j}
separately. With a slight abuse of notation, we write

(6.138)

\displaystyle J_{1}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w):=\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\phi(s-\varsigma)\,,

\displaystyle J_{2}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w):=\left(\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\phi(s-\varsigma)\,,

\displaystyle J_{3}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w):=\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w)\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w)\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}(1-\phi(s-\varsigma))\,,

to emphasize the dependence on
w^{1}
and
w^{2}
. Also, throughout the proof we assume that
|\beta|\leqslant\rho\leqslant\rho_{0}
.

We first analyze the part corresponding to
J_{1}
. To that end, we set

a_{j}=a_{j}(s+i\beta):=\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta;\,w^{j})\,,\quad\textup{and}\quad b_{j}=b_{j}(s+i\beta)=\zeta(s+i\beta;\,w^{j})\,,

and point out that

\displaystyle J_{1}(x,s+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{1}(x,s+i\beta;\,w^{2})=\frac{(a_{1}x+\varepsilon b_{1})^{\perp}}{|a_{1}x+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{(a_{2}x+\varepsilon b_{2})^{\perp}}{|a_{2}x+\varepsilon b_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=(a_{1}-a_{2})^{\perp}\frac{x}{|xa_{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+(b_{1}-b_{2})^{\perp}\frac{\varepsilon}{|xa_{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle+(xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{2})^{\perp}\left(\frac{1}{|xa_{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{|xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)+(xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{2})^{\perp}\left(\frac{1}{|xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{|xa_{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right).

Moreover, it follows that

(6.139)

|a_{1}-a_{2}|\lesssim\|(w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\|(w_{2}^{1}-w_{2}^{2})[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},

and that

(6.140)

|b_{1}-b_{2}|\lesssim|l-\ell|\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\|(w_{3}^{1}-w_{3}^{2})[l,\cdot+i\beta]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|l-\ell|\,\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Then, arguing exactly as we did in (
6.71
)-(
6.73
), we conclude that

(6.141)

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{1}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{1}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\|w_{4}^{2}[l,\cdot]\|_{\rho}\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Next, to analyze the part corresponding to
J_{2}
, we rely on Lemma
6.1
. First of all, we set

(6.142)

\displaystyle p=p(\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w^{1}):=\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,

(6.143)

\displaystyle q=q(\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w^{1}):=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,

(6.144)

\displaystyle r=r(\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w^{2}):=\tilde{d}(s-\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

(6.145)

\displaystyle t=t((\varsigma,s+i\beta;\,w^{2}):=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

and observe that

(6.146)

\displaystyle J_{2}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s-\varsigma,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})=-\left(\frac{p^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q^{\perp}}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\phi(s-\varsigma)\,.

Having this expression at hand, we argue as in the proof of Lemma
6.8
. First, observe that

|p-q-r+t|\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Then, by (
6.76
) (see also the proof of (
6.115
)), we get that

(6.147)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{p^{\perp}-q^{\perp}-r^{\perp}+t^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,\phi(s-\varsigma)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Likewise, we have that

(6.148)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2p\langle p,p-q+r-t\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\right|\lesssim\frac{\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}}{|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,,

and so that

(6.149)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{2p\langle p,p-q+r-t\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\,\phi(s-\varsigma)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

On the other hand, we have that

|p-q|+|r-t|\lesssim|s-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\,,

and that

|q-t|+|p-r|\lesssim\big(|s-\varsigma|+\varepsilon|l-\ell|\big)\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Using then the shortened notation introduced in (
6.134
), we conclude that

(6.150)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\rm K}(p,q,r,t)\,\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Combining (
6.147
), (
6.149
) and (
6.150
) with Lemma
6.1
, we then obtain that

(6.151)

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{2}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Finally, arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.9
, it straightforward to check that

(6.152)

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\left\lVert\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{3}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{3}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

The result follows combining (
6.141
), (
6.151
) and (
6.152
).
∎

We are missing the bound of the corresponding Hölder seminorm. Using the notation introduced in (
6.138
), we first prove the following:

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

(6.153)

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{1}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{1}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Proof.

We start pointing out that there is no loss of generality in considering
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{T}
with
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leqslant\frac{1}{10}
. The case where
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\geqslant\frac{1}{10}
immediately follows from Lemma
6.11
. Also, we introduce the shortened notation

a_{j}^{h}:=a(s_{j}+i\beta;\,w^{j})=\digamma^{\prime}(s_{j}+i\beta;\,w^{h})\,,\quad b_{j}^{h}:=b(s_{j}+i\beta;\,w^{h})=\zeta(s_{j}+i\beta;\,w^{h})\,,\quad j,\,h\in\{1,2\}\,,

and observe that

(6.154)

\displaystyle J_{1}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{1}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})

\displaystyle-J_{1}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+J_{1}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{((s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{1}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{1}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{1}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{((s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{1}^{2})^{\perp}}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{1}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{1}^{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg)\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\big(g_{a}(s_{1}-\varsigma,a_{1}^{1})-g_{a}(s_{1}-\varsigma,a_{1}^{2})-g_{a}(s_{2}-\varsigma,a_{2}^{1})+g_{a}(s_{2}-\varsigma,a_{2}^{2})\big)

\displaystyle+\big(g_{b}(s_{1}-\varsigma,b_{1}^{1})-g_{b}(s_{1}-\varsigma,b_{1}^{2})-g_{b}(s_{2}-\varsigma,b_{2}^{1})+g_{b}(s_{2}-\varsigma,b_{2}^{2})\big)\Big\}\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)

\displaystyle+\bigg\{\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg\}\big(\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big)\,,

with
