g_{a}(s_{j}-\varsigma,a_{j}^{h}):=\frac{((s_{j}-\varsigma)a_{j}^{h}+\varepsilon b_{j}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{j}-\varsigma)a_{j}^{h}+\varepsilon b_{j}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\quad\textup{and}\quad g_{b}(s_{j}-\varsigma,b_{j}^{h}):=\frac{((s_{j}-\varsigma)a_{j}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{j}^{h})^{\perp}}{|(s_{j}-\varsigma)a_{j}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{j}^{h}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,,\quad j,h\in\{1,2\}\,.

Having this decomposition at hand, we can estimate the convolution with the double differences in the first bracket using Lemmas
6.2
and
6.4
, and arguing as we did in the proof of Lemma
5.2
. Through the rest of the proof we assume that
|\beta|\leqslant\rho
. First, note that

(6.155)

\displaystyle|a_{1}^{1}-a_{1}^{2}-a_{2}^{1}+a_{2}^{2}|=|\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})-\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+\digamma^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta;w^{2})|

(6.156)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big[(w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\big]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}+\big[(w_{2}^{1}-w_{2}^{2})[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\big]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\big)

(6.157)

\displaystyle\qquad+|s_{1}-s_{2}|\big(\left\lVert(w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\left\lVert(w_{2}^{1}-w_{2}^{2})[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\big)

(6.158)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Likewise, we have that (see (
6.139
))

(6.159)

\displaystyle|a_{1}^{1}-a_{1}^{2}|+|a_{2}^{1}-a_{2}^{2}|\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},

and that

|a_{1}^{1}-a_{2}^{1}|+|a_{1}^{2}-a_{2}^{2}|\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Combining (
6.22
) with Lemma
6.4
and these estimates, we conclude that

(6.160)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(g_{a}(s_{1}-\varsigma,a_{1}^{1})-g_{a}(s_{1}-\varsigma,a_{1}^{2})-g_{a}(s_{2}-\varsigma,a_{2}^{1})+g_{a}(s_{2}-\varsigma,a_{2}^{2})\Big)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

(6.161)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,,\quad\textup{ for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

On the other hand, we have that

(6.162)

\displaystyle|b_{1}^{1}-b_{1}^{2}-b_{2}^{1}+b_{2}^{2}|=|\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})-\zeta(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+\zeta(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})|

(6.163)

\displaystyle\quad=\left|\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(w_{3}^{1}-w_{3}^{2})[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)\dot{\Gamma}(s_{1}+i\beta)^{\perp}-\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(w_{3}^{1}-w_{3}^{2})[\mu,s_{2}+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)\dot{\Gamma}(s_{2}+i\beta)^{\perp}\right|

(6.164)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|l-\ell|\,\bigg(\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\big[(w_{3}^{1}-w_{3}^{2})[l,\cdot+i\beta]\big]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}

(6.165)

\displaystyle\qquad+|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert(w_{3}^{1}-w_{3}^{2})[l,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\bigg)

(6.166)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|l-\ell|\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Also, it follows that (see (
6.140
))

(6.167)

\displaystyle|b_{1}^{1}-b_{1}^{2}|+|b_{2}^{1}-b_{2}^{2}|\lesssim|l-\ell|\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},

and that

|b_{1}^{1}-b_{2}^{1}|+|b_{1}^{2}-b_{2}^{2}|\lesssim|l-\ell||s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining (
6.22
), Lemma
6.4
, and the lower bound (
6.72
) with these estimates, we conclude that

(6.168)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(g_{b}(s_{1}-\varsigma,b_{1}^{1})-g_{b}(s_{1}-\varsigma,b_{1}^{2})-g_{b}(s_{2}-\varsigma,b_{2}^{1})+g_{b}(s_{2}-\varsigma,b_{2}^{2})\Big)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

(6.169)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,,\quad\textup{ for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Finally, to estimate the convolution with the remaining term in (
6.154
), we point out that

(6.170)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\big|\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big|

(6.171)

\displaystyle\quad=\left|\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\left|\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(\lambda(s_{1}-s_{2})+s_{2}-\varsigma){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\right||s_{1}-s_{2}|\,.

Then, taking into account (
6.110
) and arguing as in the proof of (
6.141
), one can see that

(6.172)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\bigg\{\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{1}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{((s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2})^{\perp}}{|(s_{2}-\varsigma)a_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon b_{2}^{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg\}\big(\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

(6.173)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,,\quad\textup{ for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

The result follows from (
6.161
), (
6.169
) and (
6.173
).
∎

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

(6.174)

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{2}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Proof.

We start pointing out that there is no loss of generality in considering
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{T}
with
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leqslant\frac{1}{32}
. The case where
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\geqslant\frac{1}{32}
follows from Lemma
6.11
. Now, observe that

(6.175)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Big(J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})

\displaystyle-J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\Big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\leqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\big(J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{2}|\leqslant 4|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\big(J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\Big(J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})

\displaystyle-J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\Big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

The first two terms can be handled as we did in Lemma
6.11
to prove (
6.151
), the main difference being the domain where we are integrating. This difference is precisely what makes appear the extra factor
|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and allows us to prove that

(6.176)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\leqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\big(J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{2}|\leqslant 4|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\big(J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Having this estimate at hand, we are just missing to deal with the last term on the right-hand side of (
6.175
). To that end, we first split the kernel there as

(6.177)

\displaystyle J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{2}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})-J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+J_{2}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})

(6.178)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.179)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.180)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\Bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)

(6.181)

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg(\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.182)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\Bigg)\big(\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big)\,.

The part corresponding to the second parenthesis can be analyzed using Lemma
6.1
. We set

(6.183)

\displaystyle p=p(\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1}):=\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,

\displaystyle q=q(\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1}):=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,

\displaystyle r=r(\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2}):=\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

\displaystyle t=t(\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2}):=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

and point out that

(6.184)

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.185)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\big(d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{\big(\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)^{\perp}}{\big|\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\Bigg|\big|\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big|

(6.186)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{p^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q^{\perp}}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\big|\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big|

(6.187)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{p^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q^{\perp}}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right|\left|\int_{0}^{1}\phi^{\prime}(\lambda(s_{1}-s_{2})+s_{2}-\varsigma){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\right||s_{1}-s_{2}|

Taking into account (
6.110
), one can argue as in the proof of (
6.151
) and conclude that

(6.188)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\left(\frac{p^{\perp}}{|p|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q^{\perp}}{|q|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r^{\perp}}{|r|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t^{\perp}}{|t|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)\big(\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)-\phi(s_{2}-\varsigma)\big)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

To analyze the remaining term, we set

(6.189)

\displaystyle p_{1}:=\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,\qquad

\displaystyle p_{2}:=\tilde{d}(s_{1}-\varsigma,s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

\displaystyle q_{1}:=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,\qquad

\displaystyle q_{2}:=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{1}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

\displaystyle r_{1}:=\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,

\displaystyle r_{2}:=\tilde{d}(s_{2}-\varsigma,s_{2}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

\displaystyle t_{1}:=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{1})\,,

\displaystyle t_{2}:=d_{l,\ell}^{\varepsilon}(s_{2}+i\beta,\varsigma+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,.

The corresponding kernel can then be written as

(6.190)

-\bigg(\frac{p_{1}^{\perp}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q_{1}^{\perp}}{|q_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r_{1}^{\perp}}{|r_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t_{1}^{\perp}}{|t_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p_{2}^{\perp}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{q_{2}^{\perp}}{|q_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{r_{2}^{\perp}}{|r_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t_{2}^{\perp}}{|t_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)\,,

and then, using (
6.15
) twice, we can rewrite this kernel in a more suitable way for our purposes. Indeed, note that

(6.191)

\displaystyle\frac{p_{1}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q_{1}}{|q_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r_{1}}{|r_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t_{1}}{|t_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p_{2}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{q_{2}}{|q_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{r_{2}}{|r_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t_{2}}{|t_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.192)

\displaystyle\quad=\biggl(\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\biggr)

(6.193)

\displaystyle\qquad+\biggl(\frac{2p_{1}\langle p_{1},q_{1}-p_{1}+r_{1}-t_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}-\frac{2p_{2}\langle p_{2},q_{2}-p_{2}+r_{2}-t_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\biggr)

(6.194)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((t_{1}-r_{1})\frac{\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-(t_{2}-r_{2})\frac{\langle p_{2}-r_{2},p_{2}+r_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\right)

(6.195)

\displaystyle\qquad+\biggl(2p_{1}\frac{\langle q_{1}-p_{1}-t_{1}+r_{1},p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\langle p_{1}-q_{1},p_{1}+q_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}|q_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.196)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-2p_{2}\frac{\langle q_{2}-p_{2}-t_{2}+r_{2},p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\langle p_{2}-q_{2},p_{2}+q_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}|q_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\biggr)

(6.197)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(q_{1}\frac{\langle q_{1}-t_{1}-p_{1}+r_{1},q_{1}-p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-q_{2}\frac{\langle q_{2}-t_{2}-p_{2}+r_{2},q_{2}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.198)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(2(q_{1}-p_{1})\frac{\langle q_{1}-p_{1}-t_{1}+r_{1},p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-2(q_{2}-p_{2})\frac{\langle q_{2}-p_{2}-t_{2}+r_{2},p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.199)

\displaystyle\qquad-\biggl(q_{1}\langle t_{1}-r_{1},q_{1}+p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|t_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.200)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-q_{2}\langle t_{2}-r_{2},q_{2}+p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|t_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)\biggr)

(6.201)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(q_{1}\frac{\langle t_{1}-r_{1},t_{1}-q_{1}+r_{1}-p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-q_{2}\frac{\langle t_{2}-r_{2},t_{2}-q_{2}+r_{2}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|q_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.202)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((q_{1}-t_{1})\frac{\langle t_{1}-r_{1},t_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-(q_{2}-t_{2})\frac{\langle t_{2}-r_{2},t_{2}+r_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|t_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)\,.

The parts corresponding to the first two terms on the right hand side need to be treated as singular integrals. We can estimate them arguing as we did in Lemma
6.8
to prove (
6.117
) and (
6.130
). On the other hand, the parts corresponding to the rest of the terms can be directly treated reasoning as we did in Lemma
6.8
to prove (
6.135
). We show in detail how to deal with the parts corresponding to the first and third parenthesis on the right hand side. The corresponding part for the second one will then follow arguing as we are going to do with the first, and the rest will follow arguing as we are going to do with the third term.

We first deal with the more difficult part corresponding to the first parenthesis on the right hand side of (
6.202
). To that end, we introduce the auxiliary function

g:[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}^{2}\,,\quad\lambda\mapsto\frac{\lambda(p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1})+(1-\lambda)(p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2})}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\,,

and point out that

\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=g(1)-g(0)=\int_{0}^{1}g^{\prime}(\lambda){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\,.

Hence, it follows that

(6.203)

\displaystyle\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}-p_{2}+q_{2}+r_{2}-t_{2}}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}{\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda

(6.204)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\lambda(p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1})+(1-\lambda)(p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2})}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\Big(2\lambda|p_{1}-p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+\langle p_{2},p_{1}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\,.

We treat the part corresponding to each integral separately. First, setting

(6.205)

\displaystyle G(a+ib;\,w_{1}^{j}):=\digamma(a+ib;\,w_{1}^{j})-\Gamma(a+ib)=w_{1}^{j}[\ell,a+ib]\dot{\Gamma}(a+ib)^{\perp}\,,\quad j\in\{1,2\}\,,

(6.206)

\displaystyle a_{\lambda}=a_{\lambda}(s_{1}+i\beta):=\lambda\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+(1-\lambda)\digamma^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{2})\,,

(6.207)

\displaystyle b_{\lambda}=b_{\lambda}(s_{1}+i\beta):=\lambda\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w^{1})+(1-\lambda)\zeta(s_{1}+i\beta)\,,

we get that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}-p_{2}+q_{2}+r_{2}-t_{2}}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{G(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})-G(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})-(s_{1}-s_{2})G^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{\lambda}+\varepsilon b_{\lambda}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(s_{1}-\varsigma)(G^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})-G^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2}))}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{\lambda}+\varepsilon b_{\lambda}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=:{\rm K}_{G}^{1}(s_{1},s_{2},\varsigma,\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})+{\rm K}_{G}^{2}(s_{1},s_{2},\varsigma,\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})\,.

Here,
G^{\prime}
denotes the derivative of
G
with respect to
s
. Then, arguing as in (
6.114
), we get that

(6.208)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm K}_{G}^{1}(s_{1},s_{2},\varsigma,\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})\right|\lesssim\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|s_{1}-\varsigma|^{2}}\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,,

and so that

(6.209)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm K}_{G}^{1}(s_{1},s_{2},\varsigma,\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2}){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\right)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\frac{1}{|s_{1}-\varsigma|^{2}}{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

On the other hand, let us point out that

|G^{\prime}(s_{1}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})-G^{\prime}(s_{2}+i\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})|\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Arguing then as in the proof of (
6.116
) and using Fubini’s theorem, we get that

(6.210)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}{\rm K}_{G}^{2}(s_{1},s_{2},\varsigma,\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2}){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\rm K}_{G}^{2}(s_{1},s_{2},\varsigma,\beta;\,w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)\mathds{1}_{|s_{1}-\varsigma|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|{\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Hence, it follows that

(6.211)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}-p_{2}+q_{2}+r_{2}-t_{2}}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}{\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\left\lVert w_{4}[\ell,\cdot+i\beta]\right\rVert_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\,,\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Next, observe that

(6.212)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|p_{1}-p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}=(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{2}\big(|(w_{2}^{1}-w_{2}^{2})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]|^{2}+|(w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]|^{2}|\ddot{\Gamma}(s_{1}+i\beta)|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}\big)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(w_{3}^{2}-w_{3}^{1})[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon(s_{1}-\varsigma)\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(w_{3}^{2}-w_{3}^{1})[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)(w_{2}^{1}-w_{2}^{2})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]\,,

and that

(6.213)

\displaystyle\langle p^{2},p^{1}-p^{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}=(s_{1}-\varsigma)^{2}\Big((w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]\langle\dot{\Gamma}(s_{1}+i\beta),\ddot{\Gamma}(s_{1}+i\beta)^{\perp}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle+w_{1}^{2}[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta](w_{1}^{1}-w_{1}^{2})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]|\ddot{\Gamma}(s_{1}+i\beta)|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}+w_{2}^{2}[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta](w_{2}^{1}-w_{2}^{2})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]\Big)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(w_{3}^{1}-w_{3}^{2})[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{2}[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon(s_{1}-\varsigma)\bigg(w_{2}^{2}[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(w_{3}^{2}-w_{3}^{1})[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)

\displaystyle+(w_{2}^{2}-w_{2}^{1})[\ell,s_{1}+i\beta]\left(\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}^{2}[\mu,s_{1}+i\beta]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)\bigg)\,.

Also, let us point out that

(6.214)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\lambda(p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1})+(1-\lambda)(p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2})}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\Big(2\lambda|p_{1}-p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+\langle p_{2},p_{1}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda

(6.215)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\lambda(p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1})}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{\lambda}+\varepsilon b_{\lambda}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\Big(2\lambda|p_{1}-p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+\langle p_{2},p_{1}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda

(6.216)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(1-\lambda)(p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2})}{|(s_{1}-\varsigma)a_{\lambda}+\varepsilon b_{\lambda}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\Big(2\lambda|p_{1}-p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+\langle p_{2},p_{1}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\,.

Taking into account (
6.212
) and (
6.213
), the part corresponding to each integral above can be estimated arguing as in the proofs of (
6.130
) and (
6.211
). The key here is that

|b_{\lambda}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}|l-\ell|\,,\quad\textup{for all }\lambda\in[0,1]\,.

This can be proved by arguing exactly as in the proof of (
6.72
). Once we have this estimate, we can systematically use Lemma
6.2
and get that

(6.217)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\lambda(p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1})+(1-\lambda)(p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2})}{|\lambda p_{1}+(1-\lambda)p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}

\displaystyle\times\Big(2\lambda|p_{1}-p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}+\langle p_{2},p_{1}-p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big){\,\mathrm{d}}\lambda\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Then, combining (
6.211
) and (
6.217
) we conclude that

(6.218)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\bigg(\frac{p_{1}-q_{1}-r_{1}+t_{1}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{p_{2}-q_{2}-r_{2}+t_{2}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

We now deal with part corresponding to the third term on (
6.202
). By direct computations,

(6.219)

\displaystyle(t_{1}-r_{1})\frac{\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-(t_{2}-r_{2})\frac{\langle p_{2}-r_{2},p_{2}+r_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

(6.220)

\displaystyle\quad=((t_{1}-t_{2})-(r_{1}-r_{2}))\frac{\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}

(6.221)

\displaystyle\qquad+(t_{2}-r_{2})\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{1}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}-\frac{1}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.222)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{t_{2}-r_{2}}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\Big(\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}-\langle p_{2}-r_{2},p_{2}+r_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big)

(6.223)

\displaystyle\quad=((t_{1}-t_{2})-(r_{1}-r_{2}))\frac{\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}

(6.224)

\displaystyle\qquad+(t_{2}-r_{2})\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{\langle p_{2}-p_{1},p_{2}+p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+\frac{\langle r_{2}-r_{1},r_{2}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.225)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{t_{2}-r_{2}}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\Big(\langle p_{1}-p_{2},p_{1}+p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}+\langle r_{2}-r_{1},r_{2}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big)\,.

Then, arguing as in the proof of (
6.75
), we get that

|t_{1}-t_{2}-r_{1}+r_{2}|\lesssim|s_{2}-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,.

Likewise, it follows that

|p_{2}-p_{1}|\lesssim\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}(|s_{1}-\varsigma|+\varepsilon|l-\ell|)\,,\quad|r_{2}-r_{1}|\lesssim\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}(|s_{2}-\varsigma|+\varepsilon|l-\ell|)\,.

Moreover, observe that
|s_{1}-\varsigma|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{1}|
implies that

\frac{2}{3}|s_{1}-\varsigma|\leqslant|s_{2}-\varsigma|\leqslant\frac{4}{3}|s_{1}-\varsigma|\,.

Thus, we can estimate the right hand side on (
6.225
) as

(6.226)

\displaystyle\bigg|((t_{1}-t_{2})-(r_{1}-r_{2}))\frac{\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}

(6.227)

\displaystyle\quad+(t_{2}-r_{2})\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\frac{\langle p_{2}-p_{1},p_{2}+p_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}+\frac{\langle r_{2}-r_{1},r_{2}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|r_{1}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\right)

(6.228)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{t_{2}-r_{2}}{|p_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}|r_{2}|^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}}\Big(\langle p_{1}-p_{2},p_{1}+p_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}+\langle r_{2}-r_{1},r_{2}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\Big)\bigg|

(6.229)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|s_{1}-\varsigma||s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{1}-\varsigma|^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}\,,

and arguing exactly as in (
6.135
), we conclude that

(6.230)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\bigg((t_{1}-r_{1})\frac{\langle p_{1}-r_{1},p_{1}+r_{1}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle-(t_{2}-r_{2})\frac{\langle p_{2}-r_{2},p_{2}+r_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}|r_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

The rest of the terms on the right hand side of (
6.202
) can be handled arguing in a similar way. We conclude that

(6.231)

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|\varsigma-s_{1}|\geqslant 3|s_{1}-s_{2}|}\bigg(\frac{p_{1}^{\perp}}{|p_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{q_{1}^{\perp}}{|q_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{r_{1}^{\perp}}{|r_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{t_{1}^{\perp}}{|t_{1}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{2}^{\perp}}{|p_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{q_{2}^{\perp}}{|q_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}+\frac{r_{2}^{\perp}}{|r_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}-\frac{t_{2}^{\perp}}{|t_{2}|_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\bigg)\phi(s_{1}-\varsigma)w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert w^{1}-w^{2}\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}},\quad\textup{for }|\beta|\leqslant\rho\,.

Taking into account (
6.175
), the result follows combining (
6.176
), (
6.188
) and (
6.231
).
∎

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
C_{2}>0
be as in Lemma
6.6
. If
w\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}
satisfies (
3.26
) with
{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\leqslant C_{2}
, (
6.45
) and (
6.46
), then

(6.232)

\displaystyle\sup_{l,\ell\in[-1,1]}

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{|\beta|\leqslant\rho}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big(J_{3}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{1})-J_{3}(\cdot-\varsigma,\cdot+i\beta;\,w^{2})\big)w_{4}^{2}[\ell,\varsigma+i\beta]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\bigg]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{1}-w^{2}\|_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{4}_{\rho}}.

Proof.

The result immediately follows from the chain rule and the lower bound in (
6.9
).
∎

At this point, we can finally conclude the proof of Proposition
3.4
.

Proof of (
3.28
).

Taking into account (
6.44
) and (
6.48
), (
3.28
) immediately follows from the decomposition in (
6.137
) and Lemmas
6.10
,
6.11
,
6.12
,
6.13
and
6.14
.
∎

7.
Convergence estimates

This section is devoted to prove Proposition
3.6
and Lemma
3.8
. We start by proving Lemma
3.8
. To that end, we start with the following preliminary result:

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\rho^{\prime}\in(0,\rho)
. Then the embedding
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
is compact. Furthermore, the
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
–norm is lower semicontinuous with respect to convergence in the distributional sense.

Proof.

By Cauchy’s integral theorem, it holds that

(7.1)

\displaystyle\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{C^{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho^{\prime}})}\lesssim\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{\rho},

for any
k\geqslant 0
.
Hence, by the compactness of the embedding
C^{k}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho^{\prime}})\hookrightarrow C^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho^{\prime}})
for all
k\geqslant 1
, any bounded sequence must have a convergent subsequence. The limit of this subsequence must satisfy the Cauchy–Riemann equations distributionally. It is therefore holomorphic, and it belongs to
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
.

To prove the lower semicontinuity, we take a sequence
(u_{n})_{n}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
converging in the distributional sense to
u\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
. Then, it follows that

(7.2)

\displaystyle\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{\rho}=\sup_{\rho^{\prime}<\rho}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}=\sup_{\rho^{\prime}<\rho}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left\lVert u_{n}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\leqslant\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\rho^{\prime}<\rho}\left\lVert u_{n}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left\lVert u_{n}\right\rVert_{\rho},

where we have used the compactness in the second step.
∎

For later purposes, we also set the notation

(7.3)

\displaystyle K_{0}^{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}[w](s):=K_{0}\big[w_{1}[\ell_{1},\cdot],w_{4}[\ell_{2},\cdot]\big](s)\,,

(7.4)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w](s):=K_{\varepsilon}\left[w_{1}[\ell,\cdot],w_{2}[\ell,\cdot],\int_{l}^{\ell}(1+w_{3}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu,w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\right](s)\,.

Moreover, through the rest of this section, we assume that
w\in\mathcal{Z}_{\rho}
and that it satisfies

(7.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{j}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}<\mathcal{C}_{0}\quad\textup{and}\quad\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}\leqslant\mathcal{C}_{3}<\infty\,.

Note that the constants
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\mathcal{C}_{3}
were chosen in Section
3
, with
\mathcal{C}_{0}
being sufficiently small. Likewise, we assume that
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
. Also, let us stress that, for simplicity, here and during the rest of the section, we omit the dependence on
t
.

Finally, we set

(7.6)

\displaystyle\overline{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell}(s):=\dot{\Gamma}(s)+w_{2}[\ell,s]\dot{\Gamma}(s)^{\perp}+w_{1}[\ell,s]\ddot{\Gamma}(s)^{\perp}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell}(s,t)=\overline{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell}(s)^{\perp}\,,

for the tangent and normal of
\gamma_{\varepsilon,\ell}(s)
.

The proof of both results relies on the following two lemmas, whose proofs we postpone.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
l,\ell\in[-1,1]
with
l\neq\ell
. Then, for all
0\leqslant\rho<\rho_{0}
, it holds that

(7.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\left\lVert\bar{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell}(\cdot)\cdot(K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w]-K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w])\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})}=0\,,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\left\lVert\bar{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell}(\cdot)\cdot\left(K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w]-K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]\right)+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sign}(l-\ell)w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\rho})}=0\,.

Lemma 7.3
.

There exists a constant
{\mathcal{C}}_{4}\in(0,\mathcal{C}_{0}]
, depending only on
\Gamma
, such that, for all
l,\ell,\ell_{1}\in[-1,1]
with
l\neq\ell
, and every
0<\rho<\rho_{0}
, if
w\in\mathcal{Z}_{\rho}
satisfies

\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{j}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}<{\mathcal{C}}_{4}\quad\textup{and}\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{5}:=\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}<\infty\,,

then, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
, it follows that

(7.9)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]\right\rVert_{{\rho^{\prime}}}\lesssim A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,.

Here,
A:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is some positive, decreasing function and the implicit constant depends on
\mathcal{C}_{5}
.

We first prove Lemma
3.8
.

Proof of Lemma
3.8
.

First of all, observe that by (
7.7
) and (
7.8
)

(7.10)

\displaystyle K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w]=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]-\frac{\digamma^{\prime}(s)}{2|\digamma(s)|^{2}}w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\,.

Moreover, by Proposition
3.4
, we know that
K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}
is Lipschitz in
w
, uniformly in
\varepsilon
, and by Lemma
7.1
, this also holds for the limit. On the other hand, the second term on the right-hand side is Lipschitz in
w
by Lemma
5.2
. Hence, the statements about
K_{0}
follow.

Finally, the statements about
U_{0}^{\,\mathbf{n}}
and
U_{0}^{\,\mathbf{s}}
follow from Proposition
3.3
, the statements about
K_{0}
, and the fact that
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
is an algebra.
∎

We move on to the proof of Proposition
3.6
and prove the following result, which, taking into account
3.8
, directly implies Proposition
3.6
by integrating in
\ell
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{4}
be as in Lemma
7.3
and let
l,\ell,\ell_{1}\in[-1,1]
with
l\neq\ell
. For every
0\leqslant\rho<\rho_{0}
, if
w\in\mathcal{Z}_{\rho}
satisfies

\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{j}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}<{\mathcal{C}}_{4}\quad\textup{and}\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{5}:=\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{4}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}<\infty\,,

then, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho<\rho_{0}
, it follows that

(7.11)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell_{1}}(\cdot)\cdot\left(K_{0}^{\ell_{1},\ell}[w]-K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]\right)\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\varepsilon A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,,

and that

(7.12)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell_{1}}(\cdot)\cdot\left(K_{0}^{\ell_{1},\ell}[w]-K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]\right)+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sign}(l-\ell)w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\varepsilon A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,.

Here,
A:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is some positive, decreasing function and the implicit constants depend on
\mathcal{C}_{5}
.

Proof of Proposition
7.4
.

We first point out that the proof is reduced to showing the existence of some positive, decreasing function
A
such that, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
,

(7.13)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bar{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell}(\cdot)\cdot\left(K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w]-K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]\right)\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\varepsilon A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,,

and

(7.14)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bar{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell}(\cdot)\cdot\left(K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w]-K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]\right)+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sign}(l-\ell)w_{4}[\ell,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\varepsilon A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,.

Indeed, by Lemma
3.8
, we get that

(7.15)

\displaystyle\left\lVert K_{0}^{\ell_{1},\ell}[w]-K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w]\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{1}[\ell_{1},\cdot]-w_{1}[\ell,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}+\left\lVert\partial_{s}(w_{1}[\ell_{1},\cdot]-w_{1}[\ell,\cdot])\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}

(7.16)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\|w_{1}[\ell_{1},\cdot]-w_{1}[\ell,\cdot]\|_{\rho}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\left\lVert\int_{\ell}^{\ell_{1}}(1+w_{3}[\mu,\cdot]){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right\rVert_{\rho}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\,.

Likewise, observe that it follows directly from the definition that

(7.17)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell_{1}}-\bar{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}+\left\lVert\overline{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell_{1}}-\bar{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\left\lVert w_{1}[\ell_{1},\cdot]-w_{1}[\ell,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}+\left\lVert\partial_{s}(w_{1}[\ell_{1},\cdot]-w_{1}[\ell,\cdot])\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\,.

Hence, if we show that (
7.13
) and (
7.14
) hold, the result follows from the triangle inequality and the fact that
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho}
is an algebra.

We now prove (
7.13
) and (
7.14
). First, taking into account (
3.27
) and Lemma
7.1
, we infer that the convergence in (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) actually holds in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
. On the other hand, by Lemma
7.3
, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
and all
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon]
, it follows that

(7.18)

\displaystyle\left\lVert K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w]-K_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\ell,l}[w]\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\varepsilon A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,,

where
A
is some positive, decreasing function. Using again the triangle inequality, and sending
\varepsilon^{\prime}\to 0^{+}
, we conclude that (
7.13
) and (
7.14
) hold, and thus the result follows.
∎

Finally, we prove Lemmas
7.2
and
7.3
.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We will reuse
\digamma
and
\zeta
, which were defined in (
6.42
) and (
6.43
).
Note that
\digamma^{\prime}(s)=\bar{e}_{\mathbf{s}}^{\ell}
and
\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}=\bar{e}_{\mathbf{n}}^{\ell}
.

We consider the single-layer potential

(7.19)

\displaystyle\Psi(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\log|x-\digamma(\varsigma)|w_{4}[\ell,\varsigma]{\,\mathrm{d}}\varsigma\,,

and point out that it is a harmonic function, except on the curve
\mathbf{C}:=\digamma(\cdot)
. Moreover, by definition, it follows that

(7.20)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w](s)=\nabla^{\perp}\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))\,,\quad\textup{for all }\varepsilon>0\,.

Note that
\mathbf{C}
is an analytic curve with (non-normalized) tangent and normal vectors
\digamma^{\prime}(s)
and
\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}
. Also, since
\Gamma
is parametrized by arclength and (
7.5
) holds, we have that
\digamma^{\prime}\neq 0
. Moreover, by the orientation chosen for the curve
\Gamma
and (
7.5
), we get that
\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}
is the inner normal of the region enclosed by
\mathbf{C}
. In particular, this implies that
\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s)
lies inside the region enclosed by
\mathbf{C}
if
l>\ell
and outside if
l<\ell
.

By classical potential theory (see e.g.
[
19
, Chapter 3]
),
\Psi
is smooth up to the boundary in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\mathbf{C}
and continuous in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Moreover, using that the tangential derivative of
\Psi
exists at the boundary, it follows that

(7.21)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}\cdot K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w](s)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\digamma^{\prime}(s)\cdot\nabla\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))

\displaystyle=\digamma^{\prime}(s)\cdot\nabla\Psi(\digamma(s))=\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}\cdot K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w](s)\,,

and this limit is uniform in
s\in\mathbb{T}
. The tangential part of the velocity is more subtle, because the normal derivative of
\Psi
on
\mathbf{C}
does not exist; instead, the inner and the outer normal derivative exist, but are in general different and are related to
K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}
through the formulas

(7.22)

\displaystyle\partial_{n}^{+}\Psi(\digamma(s))=\frac{1}{2}\frac{w_{4}[\ell,s]}{|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|}-\frac{\digamma^{\prime}(s)}{|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|}\cdot K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w](s)\,,

(7.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{n}^{-}\Psi(\digamma(s))=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{w_{4}[\ell,s]}{|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|}-\frac{\digamma^{\prime}(s)}{|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|}\cdot K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w](s)\,,

where
\partial_{n}^{+}
and
\partial_{n}^{-}
denote the outer and inner normal derivative of
\Psi
at
\mathbf{C}
, and the extra denominator is due to the fact that
\mathbf{C}
is not parametrized by arc-length in (
7.19
).

Therefore, using that
\frac{\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}}{|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|}\cdot\nabla\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))
converges to the inner normal derivative if
l>\ell
and to the outer normal derivative if
l<\ell
, we see that

(7.24)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\digamma^{\prime}(s)\cdot K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w](s)=-\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}\cdot\nabla\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))

\displaystyle=-|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\digamma^{\prime}(s)^{\perp}}{|\digamma^{\prime}(s)|}\cdot\nabla\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))

\displaystyle=\digamma^{\prime}(s)\cdot K_{0}^{\ell,\ell}[w](s)+\frac{\operatorname{sign}(l-\ell)}{2}w_{4}[\ell,s]\,,

and this limit is also uniform in
\mathbb{T}
. Combining (
7.21
) and (
7.24
), the result immediately follows.
∎

To prove Lemma
7.3
we only need to consider the case
\ell=0
and
l=1
. Otherwise, we just have to replace
\varepsilon
by
\varepsilon|l-\ell|
and
w_{3}
by
w_{3}[(l-\ell)\cdot,\cdot]
. We thus fix from now on
\ell=0
and
l=1
. We need the following auxiliary result:

Lemma 7.5
.

The vectors
\digamma^{\prime}(s)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s)
and
\zeta(s)
are linearly independent and therefore the matrix

(7.25)

\displaystyle B:=\begin{pmatrix}\digamma^{\prime}(s)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s)\,,&\zeta(s)\end{pmatrix}^{-1}

is well defined. Moreover, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho<\rho_{0}
, it holds that

(7.26)

\displaystyle\left\lVert B\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}+\left\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}B\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,.

Here,
A:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is some positive, decreasing function.

Proof.

First of all, taking into account (
6.42
) and (
6.43
), it is straightforward to check that

\left\lVert\digamma^{\prime}+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}+\left\lVert\zeta\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}+\left\lVert\zeta^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho-\rho^{\prime}}\,,

for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho\leqslant\rho_{0}
. Therefore, using the formula
B^{-1}=\frac{1}{\det B}\,B^{T}
, we see that we only need to check that the complexification of the inverse determinant is bounded from below. This can be proved arguing as in Section
6
. Indeed, taking into account (
7.5
) and the fact that
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
, we get that, for all
s\in\mathbb{T}
and all
|\beta|\leqslant\rho
,

(7.27)

\displaystyle|\det\left(\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s+i\beta),\,\zeta(s+i\beta)\right)|=|\langle\digamma^{\prime}(s+i\beta)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s+i\beta),\zeta(s+i\beta)^{\perp}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}|\in\bigg[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\bigg]\,.

∎

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

First of all, note that, using the notation from the previous proof, we have that

(7.28)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w](s)\cdot\left(\digamma^{\prime}(s)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s)\right)^{\perp}=\partial_{s}\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))\,.

(7.29)

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}^{\ell,l}[w](s)\cdot\zeta(s)^{\perp}=\partial_{\varepsilon}\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))\,.

Hence, by Lemma
7.5
, we only need to show that

(7.30)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Psi(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,,

(7.31)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\partial_{s}\partial_{\varepsilon}\Psi(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,,

for some positive, decreasing function
A
.

Note that (
7.31
) immediately follows from (
7.29
), Proposition
3.4
, Cauchy integral theorem and the fact that
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
is an algebra. We hence focus on proving (
7.30
).

We are going to use that
\Psi
is a harmonic function in a neighborhood of
\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s)
. Indeed, since the curves
\digamma(\cdot)
and
\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot)
do not intersect if
w_{3}
is sufficiently small, and
\Psi
is harmonic everywhere except on the curve
\digamma(\cdot)
, this immediately follows. We want to rewrite the Laplacian in terms of the variables
\varepsilon,s
to replace the
\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}
in (
7.30
) with derivatives we control.

First note that

(7.32)

(s,\varepsilon)\mapsto\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s)=\mathbf{x}\left(s,w_{1}[0,s]+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}1+w_{3}[\mu,s]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu\right)

is a diffeomorphism to its image because
\mathbf{x}
is one and because
\left\lVert w_{3}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}<1
. We can write its inverse as

(7.33)

\displaystyle x\mapsto(\check{s}(x),\check{\varepsilon}(x))=\left(\mathbf{s}(x),\frac{\mathbf{n}(x)-w_{1}[0,\mathbf{s}(x)]}{\int_{0}^{1}1+w_{3}[\mu,\mathbf{s}(x)]{\,\mathrm{d}}\mu}\right)\,.

Then, setting

\psi(s,\varepsilon)=\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))\quad\textup{and}\quad y=\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s)\,,

we get that

(7.34)

\displaystyle 0=\Delta_{x}\Psi(y)=

\displaystyle\ |\nabla_{x}\check{s}(y)|^{2}\partial_{s}^{2}\psi(s,\varepsilon)+2(\nabla_{x}\check{s}(y)\cdot\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y))\partial_{s}\partial_{\varepsilon}\psi(s,\varepsilon)

(7.35)

\displaystyle+\Delta_{x}\check{s}(y)\partial_{s}\psi(s,\varepsilon)+\Delta_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)\partial_{\varepsilon}\psi(s,\varepsilon)+|\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)|^{2}\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}\psi(s,\varepsilon)\,,

and so, taking into account (
7.28
) and (
7.29
), that

(7.36)

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}\Psi(\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s))=\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}\psi(s,\varepsilon)

(7.37)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{|\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)|^{2}}\Big(2(\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)\cdot\nabla_{x}\check{s}(y))\partial_{s}\partial_{\varepsilon}+|\nabla_{x}\check{s}(y)|^{2}\partial_{s}^{2}+\Delta_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)\partial_{\varepsilon}+\Delta_{x}\check{s}(y)\partial_{s}\Big)\psi(s,\varepsilon)

(7.38)

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{|\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)|^{2}}\biggl(2(\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)\cdot\nabla_{x}\check{s}(y))\partial_{s}\left(K_{\varepsilon}^{0,1}[w](s)\cdot\zeta(s)^{\perp}\right)

(7.39)

\displaystyle\quad+|\nabla_{x}\check{s}(y)|^{2}\partial_{s}\left(K_{\varepsilon}^{0,1}[w]\cdot(\digamma^{\prime}(s)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s))^{\perp}\right)+\Delta_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)\left(K_{\varepsilon}^{0,1}[w](s)\cdot\zeta(s)^{\perp}\right)

(7.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta_{x}\check{s}(y)\left(K_{\varepsilon}^{0,1}[w](s)\cdot(\digamma^{\prime}(s)+\varepsilon\zeta^{\prime}(s))^{\perp}\right)\biggr)\,.

It follows from Lemma
5.2
that
\check{s}
and
\check{\varepsilon}
, evaluated at
\digamma(s)+\varepsilon\zeta(s)
lie in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
. Even more, it follows that, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
,

\|\check{s}(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))\|_{\rho^{\prime}}+\|\check{\varepsilon}(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))\|_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,,

for some positive, decreasing function
A
. Hence, by Proposition
3.4
, to conclude the proof we just have to show that
\frac{1}{|\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(y)|^{2}}
is bounded in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\rho^{\prime}}
for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
.

To see this, we note that

(7.41)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))-e_{\mathbf{n}}(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))\right\rVert_{\rho}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{|l|\leqslant 1}\left\lVert w_{j}[l,\cdot]\right\rVert_{\rho}\,,

by the definitions of
\check{\varepsilon}
,
e_{\bf{n}}
and Lemma
5.2
. Hence, since
\langle e_{\mathbf{n}},e_{\mathbf{n}}\rangle=1
by (
2.5
), using Lemma
5.2
again, we conclude that, for all
0\leqslant\rho^{\prime}<\rho
, it follows that

(7.42)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{|\nabla_{x}\check{\varepsilon}(\digamma(\cdot)+\varepsilon\zeta(\cdot))|^{2}}\right\rVert_{\rho^{\prime}}\lesssim A(\rho-\rho^{\prime})\,.

Here,
A
is some positive, decreasing function.
∎
